```
- **T4a: finish the 8000 K shear sweep** (array 4113924, `FreqSweep`). Lock-in each point as it finishes (`tools/sweep_watch.sh`); resubmit any task stopped by the time limit.
- **T4b: finish the 8000 K uniaxial x sweep** (array 4115169, `FreqSweep_x`). P = 100 ps capped at 1.1 ns (25 % on $M''$).
```

```
# Oscillatory deformation NEMD for the complex modulus of MgO, restartable in whole-period chunks.
#   MODE shear    (default): fix deform xz wiggle -> G*(w)
#   MODE uniaxial          : fix deform x  wiggle -> M*(w) = K* + 4/3 G*
# fix nvt/sllod, DeePMD potential.
#
# Required -var:  TEMP (K)  PERIOD (ps, PERIOD*1000 integer)  AMP (xz tilt or lx amplitude, Å)
#                 NTOT (production steps)  NCHUNK (periods per chunk)  NAV (steps per output bin)
#                 SEED  MODEL  DATA  NEQ (equilibration steps)  MAXCHUNKS (chunks this job)
# Outputs: stress.dat  (bin averages of the pressure tensor, Nevery=1, Nrepeat=NAV)
#          strain.dat  (instantaneous xz, lx, lz at the same steps; xz may flip by lx)
#          restart.mgo (written after every chunk; the wiggle phase is continuous because
#                       chunks are whole periods and fix deform restarts each run from tilt_start)

units           metal
boundary        p p p
atom_style      atomic
variable        MODE index shear
timestep        0.001

variable        PSTEPS equal round(${PERIOD}*1000)
variable        CHUNK  equal ${NCHUNK}*${PSTEPS}

# restart.mgo: continue production; eq.data: equilibrated, production not started; else start
variable        HAVE_RS equal is_file(restart.mgo)
variable        HAVE_EQ equal is_file(eq.data)
if "${HAVE_RS}" then "read_restart restart.mgo" elif "${HAVE_EQ}" "read_data eq.data" else "read_data ${DATA}"
change_box      all triclinic
mass            1 24.305
mass            2 16.000
neighbor        1.0 bin
neigh_modify    every 1 check yes
pair_style      deepmd ${MODEL}
pair_coeff      * *
timer           timeout ${TIMEOUT} every 1000

thermo          1000
thermo_style    custom step temp press pe pxz xz cpuremain   # pe needed by v_pe

# ---- equilibration (first job only): NVT at TEMP, then reset step so production starts at 0
if "${HAVE_RS} == 0 && ${HAVE_EQ} == 0" then &
   "velocity all create ${TEMP} ${SEED} mom yes rot yes dist gaussian" &
   "fix EQ all nvt temp ${TEMP} ${TEMP} 0.1" &
   "run ${NEQ}" &
   "unfix EQ" &
   "rese
```

**Tool `Bash`** — Write model comparison tool, inspect data file

```
mkdir -p /groups/carnegie_poc/rcohen/Downloads/T2_compress; cat > /resnick/home/recohen/Claude/tools/compare_dp_models.py <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env python
"""Compare energies, forces and virials of two DeePMD models on LAMMPS data files.
usage: compare_dp_models.py MODEL_A MODEL_B data1 [data2 ...]   (atom types 1=Mg, 2=O; triclinic ok)"""
import sys, time
import numpy as np
from deepmd.infer import DeepPot

def read_lammps_data(fn):
    L = open(fn).read().splitlines()
    xlo = ylo = zlo = 0.0; xy = xz = yz = 0.0
    for i, l in enumerate(L):
        w = l.split()
        if l.endswith('xlo xhi'): xlo, xhi = map(float, w[:2])
        elif l.endswith('ylo yhi'): ylo, yhi = map(float, w[:2])
        elif l.endswith('zlo zhi'): zlo, zhi = map(float, w[:2])
        elif l.endswith('xy xz yz'): xy, xz, yz = map(float, w[:3])
        elif l.strip().startswith('Atoms'): start = i + 2
        elif l.endswith(' atoms'): nat = int(w[0])
    rows = np.array([L[start + k].split()[:5] for k in range(nat)], float)
    rows = rows[np.argsort(rows[:, 0])]
    cell = np.array([[xhi - xlo, 0, 0], [xy, yhi - ylo, 0], [xz, yz, zhi - zlo]])
    return rows[:, 2:5], rows[:, 1].astype(int) - 1, cell
```

```
cat > /resnick/home/recohen/Claude/tools/gk_stress.lammps <<'EOF'
# Equilibrium MD for Green-Kubo viscosities of MgO (DeePMD), restartable in chunks.
# Start: read DATA (with velocities), NVT at TEMP for NEQ steps (Nose-Hoover, 0.1 ps), then NVE production.
# Production is NVE so that no thermostat enters the stress correlations (shear or bulk).
#
# Required -var: TEMP (K)  DATA  MODEL  NEQ  NTOT (production steps)  NCHUNK (steps per chunk)
#                NS (sampling interval, steps)  MAXCHUNKS  TIMEOUT (s)
# Outputs: pres.dat    step pxx pyy pzz pxy pxz pyz temp pe etotal  (instantaneous, every NS steps, bar)
#          msd.dat     step msd(Mg) msd(O)  (every 1000 steps; checks that the liquid stays liquid)
#          eq.data     end of NVT equilibration;  restart.gk  after every chunk

units           metal
boundary        p p p
atom_style      atomic
timestep        0.001

variable        HAVE_RS equal is_file(restart.gk)
variable        HAVE_EQ equal is_file(eq.data)
if "${HAVE_RS}" then "read_restart restart.gk" elif "${HAVE_EQ}" "read_data eq.data" else "read_data ${DATA}"
mass            1 24.305
mass            2 16.000
neighbor        1.0 bin
neigh_modify    every 1 che
```

```
Lock-in analysis of oscillatory-deformation MD (LAMMPS fix deform wiggle).

Fits stress and strain to  a + b t + sum_k [s_k sin(k w t) + c_k cos(k w t)]
at the KNOWN drive angular frequency w = 2 pi / period, k = 1..nharm.
Complex amplitudes at k=1 give the complex modulus
    M* = sigma_1 / eps_1 = M' + i M''      (Eq. 1)
    tan(delta) = M'' / M'                  (Eq. 2)
```

```
# synthetic: independent OU (Maxwell) processes, tau=0.1 ps, for 5 shear-like comps; check eta = G0 tau
import numpy as np
rng=np.random.default_rng(1); n=200000; dt=2e-3; tau=0.1; T=8000; V=4096*6.6868e-30; kB=1.380649e-23
G0=100e9; var=G0*kB*T/V  # <Pxy^2> in Pa^2
a=np.exp(-dt/tau); x=np.zeros((n,6)); s=np.sqrt(var*(1-a*a))
for i in range(1,n): x[i]=a*x[i-1]+s*rng.standard_normal(6)
# off-diag xy xz yz have var; diag: build traceless from 2 indep comps with same shear stat + pressure
P=np.zeros((n,6)); P[:,3:]=x[:,3:]
u,v=x[:,0],x[:,1]; # dev_xx = (2/sqrt(3))? choose dev=[u, -u/2+v*sqrt(3)/2, -u/2-v*sqrt(3)/2]*c with <dev^os sum sq> matching
c=np.sqrt(2/1.5*1.0)  # sum dev^2 = 1.5 c^2 (u^2+v^2) -> want 4 var for 5-comp equivalence: 1.5c^2*2var=4var -
… [495 more characters]
```

### Claude (10-07 04:00)

The 8000 K shear sweep and the linearity repeat were already done when I checked in. Since then I've harvested results, run two short tests, and started Green–Kubo at 8000 K and 7000 K. All jobs are running now.

**Harvest**
- **Uniaxial 100 ps point (job 4115169_5):** still running, at step 669k of 1.1M; it should finish around 09:00. A partial lock-in at 6.4 cycles gives M′ = 580.7 ± 1.2 GPa, which matches the low-frequency value of about 581 GPa. The watcher is running again and will add the final result to `lockin_results.txt`.
- **Compressed model check (T2, done):** `graph_c.pb` reproduces `graph.pb` on four 8000 K configurations.
  - Forces differ by 1 µeV/Å rms (12 µeV/Å max), against forces of about 5 eV/Å.
  - Energies differ by less than 10⁻⁷ eV/atom, and virial/V by less than 10⁻⁴ GPa.
- **Thermostat test (uniaxial, 1 THz):** I ran it as the proposed short test (2 × 15 min). It confirms the suspected thermostat artefact.

  | τ_T (ps) | M′ (GPa) | M″ (GPa) |
  |---|---|---|
  | 1.0 | 684 | 61 |
  | 0.1 (sweep) | 540 | 93 |
  | 0.02 | 609 | 73 |

  - At τ_T = 0.02 ps the run is nearly isothermal, and M′ matches K_T + (4/3)G′ ≈ 603 GPa.
  - The sweep's τ_T = 0.1 ps puts the thermal loss peak right at 1 THz, so its M″ = 93 GPa is too high. This also explains why the sweep's M′ goes up and down with frequency.
  - The sweep's low-frequency uniaxial values are isothermal (K_T), and K_S − K_T is at least 80 GPa.

**Green–Kubo, as you asked**
- **New tools:** `tools/gk_stress.lammps` and `tools/gk_viscosity.py`.
  - The LAMMPS input does a short NVT equilibration, then runs NVE and writes the full pressure tensor every 2 fs. NVE keeps the thermostat out of the bulk-viscosity result.
  - The analysis gives G(t), η₀, G′(ω), G″(ω), η′(ω), ζ₀ and the longitudinal M″(ω), with block error bars.
  - On synthetic data with a known answer of η = 10 mPa·s, it returns 9.7 ± 0.4. The LAMMPS input passed a short GPU test at 24.4 steps/s.
- **Runs:** both start from the end of the 8000 K equilibration (`8000K/FreqSweep/P1ps_r0/eq.data`). Each is 1.5 ns of NVE, about 17 h, so about 1.4 P100-days in total.
  - **8000 K:** job 4146463 in `.../4096atom/8000K/GK`.
  - **7000 K:** job 4146464 in `.../4096atom/7000K/GK`, after 20 ps of NVT at 7000 K. I'll check the atom displacement over time (`msd.dat`) to confirm it stays liquid.

I read "run 7000K" as the Green–Kubo run. I'll price an oscillatory sweep at 7000 K once the GK relaxation time tells us which frequencies matter.

The docs are updated: `ANELASTICITY_RESTART_2026-10-07.md` plus the TODO and DONE files.

**Decision for you:** how to handle the thermostat in the uniaxial sweep (TODO T12). One option is to rerun with τ_T much shorter or much longer than the drive period. The other is to rely on the NVE Green–Kubo for ζ.

### Claude (10-07 04:05)

To your question: the thermostat problem appears only under uniaxial strain. Compressing the liquid heats it adiabatically: at ε₀ = 0.03 the temperature swings by about ±300 K at the drive frequency. With Nosé–Hoover, the heat is pumped out with a lag of τ_T. When ωτ_T ≈ 1, that lag makes the heat flow out of phase with the strain. The result is an extra Debye loss between the adiabatic and isothermal moduli, of size K_S − K_T ≈ 80 GPa, which does not come from the liquid.

You are right that the fix is the thermostat mass, since Q ∝ τ_T². The number of chains does not change the coupling time, so it doesn't help. τ_T just has to sit far from the drive period. Shear is unaffected because it gives no first-order temperature change.

Let me check the setup tool and then build the 7000 K sweeps with τ_T chosen per frequency.

### Claude (10-07 04:17)

Both 7000 K sweeps are now running, alongside the Green–Kubo runs at 8000 K and 7000 K.

**The thermostat problem.** It affects only the uniaxial runs; shear produces no temperature swing at the drive frequency.
- Uniaxial strain heats and cools the liquid adiabatically, by about ±300 K at ε₀ = 0.03.
- The Nosé–Hoover thermostat removes that heat with a lag of τ_T. When the lag is comparable to the drive period, heat flow is out of phase with the strain. This adds an artificial loss: M″_th = ΔM·ωτ_T / (1 + ω²τ_T²), with ΔM = K_S − K_T ≈ 80 GPa.
- The τ_T test at 1 THz shows it directly: M″ = 61, 93 and 73 GPa for τ_T = 1, 0.1 and 0.02 ps.

Changing the thermostat mass (Q ∝ τ_T²) is indeed the fix. The number of chains doesn't change the coupling time, so it doesn't help.

**The fix I used at 7000 K.**
- τ_T is set much longer than the period, scaled with frequency so the leftover loss ΔM/(ωτ_T) stays below about 2 % of M″.
- With the thermostat that weak, viscous heating builds up, so I lowered ε₀ at high frequency to keep the temperature offset or rise to about 100 K or less.
- The uniaxial results are then adiabatic, which makes them directly comparable with the NVE Green–Kubo.

| P (ps) | ε₀ | τ_T (ps) | run (ps) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.2 | 0.010 | 2 | 40 |
| 1 | 0.015 | 10 | 40 |
| 5 | 0.020 | 250 | 100 |
| 20 | 0.03 | 10⁴ | 300 |
| 100 | 0.03 | 10⁵ | 1100 |

**7000 K sweeps.**
- **Start:** every task starts from the end of the 7000 K equilibration (`7000K/GK/eq.data`). It is still liquid: P = 183 GPa, PE flat over 2–20 ps, MSD 5 Å² at 1 ps.
- **Shear (job 4147084):** same amplitudes as at 8000 K, with the 20 ps-period run lengthened to 10 cycles.
- **Uniaxial (job 4147085):** uses the table above.
- **Cost:** about 26 P100-hours; the longest task (P = 100 ps uniaxial) takes about 13 h.
- **Watching:** a watcher will lock-in each task as it finishes.

**The old 8000 K uniaxial sweep is contaminated at every frequency.** At 50 GHz the thermostat alone accounts for about 2.5 GPa of the measured M″ = 3.9 GPa. So its M″ values and the ζ ≈ 1.8 mPa·s estimate can't be used. Its M′ at 200 GHz and below is still valid, but it is K_T (isothermal), not K_S.

**Decision for you:** should I rerun the 8000 K uniaxial sweep with the 7000 K settings? It would cost about 0.8 P100-day.

The restart doc (`ANELASTICITY_RESTART_2026-10-07.md`, §5) and the TODO list are updated.

### Claude (10-07 04:19)

The 8000 K uniaxial rerun is submitted as job 4147331, in `8000K/FreqSweep_x2`.

- **Settings:** the same ε₀ and τ_T table as at 7000 K, from ε₀ = 0.010 with τ_T = 2 ps at 0.2 ps up to ε₀ = 0.03 with τ_T = 10⁵ ps at 100 ps.
- **Start:** each task starts from its own old equilibrated state (`FreqSweep_x/P*/eq.data`), so it skips equilibration.
- **Cost:** about 0.8 P100-day; the 100 ps task takes about 13 h.
- **Watching:** a watcher will run the lock-in on each task as it finishes and add the result to `lockin_results.txt`.

The TODO list and `ANELASTICITY_RESTART_2026-10-07.md` are updated.

Also still running:
- the old 8000 K uniaxial 100 ps task, due around 09:00, which I'm keeping only for its M′ (= K_T);
- Green–Kubo at 8000 K and 7000 K, about 17 h each;
- the 7000 K shear and uniaxial sweeps.

When the Green–Kubo runs finish, I'll analyse them with `gk_viscosity.py` and compare them with both sweeps.

### Claude (10-07 05:34)

I'll write the collection and plotting tool now and test it on the results already in, then run it on the full set when the jobs finish (Green–Kubo runs about 17 h). For "viscosity from the derivative of the modulus": at low frequency G″ = ηω, so η = dG″/dω as ω → 0. I'll compute it both as slopes between adjacent sweep points and as a fit, and from the continuous GK spectrum. The same applies to dM″/dω → ζ + (4/3)η for uniaxial.

### Claude (10-07 05:37)

I've built the table, CSV and plots, and a first pass is already done with the results so far. The final version will regenerate when every job finishes; the Green–Kubo runs are the longest, about 17 h. You'll get the finished tables, plots and comparisons then.

**Where things are.** Everything is in `/resnick/home/recohen/Claude/Anelasticity/results/`:
- `results_all.md`: all results as tables, with numbered equations.
- `results_all.csv`: every sweep point plus the full Green–Kubo spectra.
- Plots as `fig_*.pdf` and `.png`: shear moduli, shear viscosity, uniaxial loss, uniaxial storage, and Green–Kubo versus time.

Fonts are Liberation Sans, which has the same metrics as Arial; Arial isn't installed on the cluster. The script that regenerates everything is `tools/anelastic_collect.py`.

**Viscosity from dG″/dω.** At low frequency G″ = η₀ω, so η₀ is the low-frequency limit of dG″/dω. I compute it three ways:
1. slopes between neighbouring sweep frequencies;
2. a weighted fit of G″ = η₀ω through the origin, using the points at 200 GHz and below;
3. the exact derivative of the Green–Kubo spectrum, dG″/dω = ∫G(t)[cos ωt − ωt sin ωt]dt.

The same is done for the uniaxial M″, whose low-frequency slope is ζ + (4/3)η.

**Interim results.** Green–Kubo has only about 130 ps of its 1.5 ns so far, so its error bars will shrink about 3×.

| | 8000 K | 7000 K |
|---|---|---|
| η₀, Green–Kubo (mPa·s) | 8.1 ± 0.8 | 10.6 ± 0.8 |
| dG″/dω at 10 GHz, Green–Kubo (mPa·s) | 8.2 ± 0.7 | 10.8 ± 0.6 |
| η₀ from sweep slope fit (mPa·s) | 7.93 ± 0.12 | 11.5 ± 0.7 (200 GHz only so far) |
| ζ₀, Green–Kubo (mPa·s) | 0.9 ± 0.2 | 1.1 ± 0.1 |
| G∞ (GPa) | 289 | 282 |
| Terminal time η₀/G∞ (ps) | 0.028 | 0.038 |

- **Agreement:** Green–Kubo and the sweeps agree point by point. At 8000 K, 1 THz, G′/G″ = 16.1/45.7 GPa (Green–Kubo) versus 16.7/44.8 GPa (sweep). At 5 THz it is 89/104 versus 87/98 GPa.
- **Slopes fall off above about 0.5 THz:** dG″/dω drops well below G″/ω (secant 2.1 mPa·s between 1 and 5 THz at 8000 K), as expected once ωτ approaches 1. Only the low-frequency limit gives η₀.
- **New uniaxial runs:** with the corrected thermostat, M″/ω at 200 GHz is 12.2 ± 1.5 mPa·s (8000 K) and 13.4 ± 2.3 mPa·s (7000 K). Both fit ζ + (4/3)η from Green–Kubo.
- **Small offset:** the 8000 K Green–Kubo run sits at ⟨T⟩ = 8112 K. In NVE the temperature is set by the energy at the end of the short NVT stage.

The restart doc (§6) and TODO list are updated.

## User (10-07 05:39)

Yes, we will want to try to get results for solid too! Hopefully will be able to extract something.

### Claude (10-07 05:39)

Good, the plan can extend to the solid. What we could measure there:

- **Oscillatory sweep:** this is the main tool for the solid. G′ becomes the elastic shear modulus, and G″/G′ = Q⁻¹ gives the internal friction. Any relaxation peaks (point defects, grain boundaries if the crystal is polycrystalline) would show up as peaks in G″(ω). Strains must be much smaller (γ₀ ≲ 0.01) to stay elastic and avoid plastic flow. Signals will be small, so runs will need to be longer.
- **Green–Kubo:** in a crystal, viscosity isn't meaningful because G(t) doesn't decay to zero. But the stress fluctuations still give the relaxing part of the modulus and its spectrum, so G″(ω) can be extracted the same way.
- **Cell size:** the box fits a B1 crystal exactly, since 4096 atoms = 8×8×8 rock-salt unit cells. If the liquid crystallizes in this box, it may form a near-perfect single crystal, or one with defects or misoriented grains. I'll check the structure, e.g. with a radial distribution function g(r), before deciding on amplitudes.

The 6000 K equilibration is still running. I'll check PE and MSD when it finishes, then submit the 6000 K sweeps. If 6000 K or a lower temperature crystallizes, I'll set up the solid runs with small amplitudes and present the cost first.

```
The 4096-atom cube fits an 8×8×8 B1 crystal exactly. RC wants solid results too.
```

```
set -e; R=/groups/carnegie_poc/rcohen/LAMMPS/MgO/4096atoms/V6.6868/4096atom; ls -la $R/4000K/GK/eq.data
for T in 5000 4000; do D=$R/${T}K/FreqSweep_solid; mkdir -p $D; cp /resnick/home/recohen/Claude/tools/anelastic_sweep.lammps $R/${T}K/GK/graph_c.pb $D/
 sed -e "s/MgOux7k/MgOso$((T/1000))k/" -e "s/eq7000.data/eq${T}.data/" -e 's/^#SBATCH --array=1-5/#SBATCH --array=1-6/' $R/7000K/FreqSweep_x/sweep.sh > $D/sweep.sh
 cat > $D/tasks.txt <<EOF
# dir TEMP PERIOD AMP NTOT NCHUNK NAV SEED NEQ MAXCHUNKS MODE TDAMP   solid (B1), gamma0 = eps0 = 0.01, 200 ps each
P0.2ps_xz ${T} 0.2 0.301434 200000 500 1 491001 0 96 shear 0.1
P1ps_xz ${T} 1 0.301434 200000 100 5 498920 0 96 shear 0.1
P5ps_xz ${T} 5 0.301434 200000 20 25 506839 0 96 shear 0.1
P0.2ps_x ${T} 0.2 0.301434 200000 500 1 491001 0 96 uniaxial 2
P1ps_x ${T} 1 0.301434 200000 100 5 498920 0 96 uniaxial 10
P5ps_x ${T} 5 0.301434 200000 20 25 506839 0 96 uniaxial 250
EOF
 cp $R/${T}K/GK/eq.data $D/eq${T}.data
 for d in $(grep -v '^#' $D/tasks.txt | awk '{print $1}'); do mkdir -p $D/$d; cp $R/${T}K/GK/eq.data $D/$d/eq.data; done
done; diff $R/7000K/FreqSweep_x/sweep.sh $R/4000K/FreqSweep_solid/sweep.sh || true
```

**Tool `Bash`** — Build perfect B1 crystal and GK job

```

import numpy as np
L=30.143432; n=8; a=L/n; T=5000.0
basis_mg=np.array([[0,0,0],[0,.5,.5],[.5,0,.5],[.5,.5,0]]); basis_o=basis_mg+np.array([.5,0,0])
pos=[];typ=[]
for i in range(n):
  for j in range(n):
    for k in range(n):
      for b in basis_mg: pos.append((np.array([i,j,k])+b)*a); typ.append(1)
      for b in basis_o:  pos.append(((np.array([i,j,k])+b)%n)*a); typ.append(2)
pos=np.array(pos)%L; typ=np.array(typ); N=len(typ)
rng=np.random.default_rng(5000); m=np.where(typ==1,24.305,16.0)
kB=8.617333e-5; conv=1.0364269e-4   # eV/(amu (A/ps)^2) -> metal: v in A/ps, m in g/mol; mv^2 [g/mol A^2/ps^2] * conv = eV
v=rng.standard_normal((N,3))*np.sqrt(kB*T/(m[:,None]*conv)); v-=(m[:,None]*v).sum(0)/m.sum()
with open('$D/B1_8x8x8.data','w') as f:
```

**Tool `Bash`** — Make sweep_watch use per-task mode

```
cd /resnick/home/recohen/Claude/tools; cp sweep_watch.sh /groups/carnegie_poc/rcohen/Downloads/sweep_watch.sh.bak_2026-10-07
python - <<'EOF'
f='sweep_watch.sh'; s=open(f).read()
s=s.replace("""    for line in $(grep -v '^#' $ROOT/tasks.txt | awk '{print $1":"$3":"$5}'); do
      i=$((i + 1))
      IFS=: read D P NTOT <<< "$line\"""","""    for line in $(grep -v '^#' $ROOT/tasks.txt | awk '{print $1":"$3":"$5":"$11}'); do
      i=$((i + 1))
      IFS=: read D P NTOT TMODE <<< "$line"
      MODE=${TMODE:-$DEFMODE}             # per-task MODE column (tasks.txt col 11) overrides ROOT:JOB:MODE""")
s=s.replace("""    IFS=: read ROOT JOB MODE <<< "$spec\"""","""    IFS=: read ROOT JOB DEFMODE <<< "$spec\"""")
open(f,'w').write(s)
EOF
grep -n "MODE" sweep_watch.sh | head
```

### Claude (10-07 09:37)

Everything you asked for is submitted and running, including 4000 K. The liquid crystallized on the way down at 5000 K, so 5000 K and 4000 K are solid runs.

**Cooling sequence so far**

| T (K) | State | Green–Kubo | Sweeps |
|---|---|---|---|
| 6000 | liquid, P = 175 GPa | 4151022 | shear 4151695, uniaxial 4151696 |
| 5000 | crystallized in NVT | 4152373 (restarted) | solid 4163773 |
| 4000 | crystal, P = 145 GPa | 4154636 | solid 4163775 |

**5000 K crystallization**
- **When:** at 8–20 ps of NVT. PE dropped 0.46 eV/atom (about the latent heat), P fell 166 → 158 GPa, and the MSD stopped growing.
- **Restart:** the first Green–Kubo run was still crystallizing, so I cancelled it 2 ps into NVE and gave it 80 ps more NVT. PE dropped once more at 70–80 ps, then stayed flat.
- **Structure:** a single B1 crystal, rotated relative to the box. There are three mutually orthogonal (200) peaks with S/N = 0.74. Because the rotated lattice doesn't fit the periodic box, the crystal must contain some defects or strain.
- **Slow defect motion:** the MSD still creeps upward.
- **4000 K** started from this crystal.

**Solid runs (your choices)**
- **Reduced sweeps** at 5000 K and 4000 K: shear and uniaxial at 5 THz, 1 THz and 200 GHz, with strain 0.01 and 200 ps each. The cost is about 0.6 P100-day per T, not the 0.4 I first quoted.
- **Perfect-crystal reference:** an 8×8×8 B1 crystal aligned with the box, Green–Kubo at 5000 K (job 4163822, about 0.7 P100-day). Comparing it with the crystal grown from the liquid separates intrinsic loss from defect loss.

**Interim liquid Green–Kubo** (8000/7000 K at about 1/3 of the run, 6000 K about 1/4)

| T (K) | η₀ (mPa·s) | ζ₀ (mPa·s) | τ₀ = η₀/G∞ (ps) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 8000 | 8.46 ± 0.27 | 0.79 ± 0.07 | 0.030 |
| 7000 | 11.6 ± 0.4 | 1.16 ± 0.09 | 0.041 |
| 6000 | 17.3 ± 0.7 | 1.6 ± 0.2 | 0.060 |

**Tool changes**
- **Green–Kubo analysis:** now subtracts the mean stress before correlating, because a crystal can hold a static residual stress. This doesn't change the liquid results.
- **Solid results are labelled separately:** the integral of G(t) is a loss term, not a viscosity, and G(0) lacks the Born term. The solid's elastic G′ comes from the sweeps.
- **New plot:** `fig_solid` shows the loss spectra and Q⁻¹ = tan δ. The viscosity plots now show only the liquids.
- **Sweep table:** gained a Q⁻¹ column.
- **Watcher:** can now handle shear and uniaxial tasks in one directory.

When the last GPU job finishes, the final tables and plots will be regenerated automatically in `Anelasticity/results/`. The restart doc (§7, §7a) and TODO are updated.

```
for k in Schottky bicrystal; do
  G=$R/GK_$k; D=$R/FreqSweep_$k
  until [ -s $G/eq.data ] && grep -q "^Velocities" $G/eq.data && [ "$(sed -n '/^Velocities/,$p' $G/eq.data | grep -c .)" -ge 3900 ]; do sleep 60; done; sleep 10

  for d in $(grep -v '^#' $D/tasks.txt | awk '{print $1}'); do mkdir -p $D/$d; cp $G/eq.data $D/$d/eq.data; done
  (cd $D && sbatch sweep.sh)
done
```

### Claude (10-07 10:08)

All three new solid cases are now in the pipeline at 5000 K:

| Case | Size | Green–Kubo job | Sweep |
|---|---|---|---|
| Perfect B1 crystal | 4096 atoms | 4163822 | 4168355, running |
| 20 bound Schottky pairs | 4056 atoms | 4169847 | submits automatically after its 50 ps NVT |
| Σ5 (310)/[001] tilt bicrystal | 3920 atoms | 4169848 | submits automatically after its 50 ps NVT |

I agree that defects are probably needed to see much in the solid.

- **Schottky pairs:** each pair is a Mg vacancy next to an O vacancy, with lower symmetry than the cubic lattice. Stress makes them hop to reorient, which should give a Debye loss peak where the frequency matches the hop rate. Isolated vacancies have cubic symmetry, so they wouldn't relax this way.
- **Bicrystal:** I built it with the grain-boundary normal along z, so the xz shear in the sweep slides the boundaries. The periodic cell holds two boundaries, 30 Å apart.
  - The boundaries hold 80 fewer atoms than a perfect crystal would, so this cell's pressure will be lower than in bulk.
  - The analysis now uses this cell's own volume, because its box shape differs from the others.
- **Existing comparison points:** the perfect crystal and the crystal grown from the liquid, which has natural defects and sits about 80 eV higher in energy.
- **Cost:** about 1.3 P100-days per defect case.

The final tables and plots (`results_all.md`, `fig_solid`) will include all three cases. The restart doc (§7b) is updated.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
- LAMMPS + DeePMD (deepmd_py311 conda env, compressed graph_c.pb, about 22–24 steps/s on a P100 for 4096 atoms).
- Oscillatory NEMD: fix deform wiggle with nvt/sllod, lock-in analysis.
- Green–Kubo (Eqs. 1–8, docstring of gk_viscosity.py):
  - G(t) from the traceless stress autocorrelation (Daivis–Evans).
  - η₀, G′ and G″ via ω∫G sin/cos.
  - Bulk ζ from ⟨δp δp⟩.
  - Longitudinal M_r(t) = K_r + 4/3 G.
- Viscosity from the slope: η₀ = lim dG″/dω, estimated three ways (secant, weighted fit through the origin for f ≤ 200 GHz, analytic GK derivative).
- Thermostat artefact in uniaxial runs: a Debye thermal loss ΔM_th = K_S − K_T ≈ 80 GPa, peaking at ωτ_T ≈ 1.
  - Fix: τ_T ≫ P, chosen per frequency, with ε₀ limited by viscous heating.
  - The thermostat mass Q ∝ τ_T² is the knob; the number of chains is irrelevant.
- Crystallization at 5000 K into B1. Structure-factor (S(k)) check. Solid GK caveats: G(0) lacks the Born term, and ∫G dt is a loss, not a viscosity. The mean stress is subtracted before correlating.
- Zener relaxation needs defects of lower symmetry than the lattice (bound Schottky pairs). Isolated vacancies do not give it.
- Σ5 (310)/[001] tilt bicrystal for grain-boundary sliding.

3. Files and Code Sections:
- **Docs** (in /resnick/home/recohen/Claude/Anelasticity):
  - ANELASTICITY_RESTART_2026-10-07.md (new): §0 checks; §1 GK; §2 T2; §3 thermostat test; §4 pitfalls; §5 7000 K sweeps (τ_T table, Eqs. 6, 7); §6 collection and plots; §7 cooling sequence; §7a solid; §7b defect crystals.

  - gk_stress.lammps:
    - Reads DATA (or restart.gk / eq.data); does `velocity all scale`, NVT 0.1 ps for NEQ steps, writes eq.data.
    - NVE production writes pres.dat (step pxx pyy pzz pxy pxz pyz temp pe etotal, every NS = 2 steps) and msd.dat.
    - Chunked with `variable NXT equal ternary(...)` and `run ${NXT} upto`; prints PRODUCTION_STEP.
  - gk_viscosity.py: analyse(P, T, V, dt, nlag) with an FFT ACF and `P = P - P.mean(axis=0)`; spectrum() with a cosine taper over the last 20 %; block errors; writes gk_G.dat and gk_spectrum.dat. Validated on synthetic Maxwell data.
  - anelastic_collect.py:
    - Configuration: SWEEPS list; GKS list of (key, root); VBOX per-run volume (bicrystal); LIQ = {'8000', '7000', '6000'}; COL colours.
    - parse_lockin(root, mode), slope_estimates, gk_analyse(root, tc, vbox), dloss_dw (analytic Eq. 5); default --gk-tc 1.0.
    - Output to Anelasticity/results/: results_all.md and results_all.csv, plus figures fig_shear_moduli, fig_shear_viscosity, fig_uniaxial_loss, fig_uniaxial_storage, fig_gk_time, fig_solid (PDF and PNG).
  - xtal_check.py: S(k) over box wavevectors.
  - build_defect_crystals.py: `schottky` and `bicrystal [--normal-z]` subcommands; writes data with velocities.

4. Errors and fixes:
- LAMMPS has no two-argument min() in formulas; replaced with ternary().
- srun from this session runs inside RC's 4-CPU sys/dash job 4055787 and hangs; use sbatch instead.

- **5000 K crystal:** a single B1 grain rotated relative to the box (three orthogonal (200) peaks, S/N = 0.74); PE −18470 eV; P = 151.8 GPa; slow MSD creep.
- **4000 K:** PE about −19000 eV, P = 145 GPa.

### Claude (10-07 12:51)

The solid sweeps at 5000 K and 4000 K are finished. All 12 tasks completed, and none hit the time limit or failed.

| T | f (THz) | G′ (GPa) | G″ (GPa) | tan δ | M′ (GPa) | M″ (GPa) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5000 K | 0.2 | 258.0 ± 1.5 | 8.1 ± 1.8 | 0.031 | 1027.8 ± 2.7 | 8.2 ± 3.2 |
| 5000 K | 1 | 270.4 ± 0.8 | 12.7 ± 0.4 | 0.047 | 1043.4 ± 1.8 | 14.7 ± 2.0 |
| 5000 K | 5 | 288.9 ± 2.6 | 13.8 ± 2.0 | 0.048 | 1096 ± 13 | 2.2 ± 6.5 |
| 4000 K | 0.2 | 277.5 ± 1.1 | 4.3 ± 0.9 | 0.016 | 1038.1 ± 1.2 | 6.8 ± 0.5 |
| 4000 K | 1 | 285.8 ± 0.2 | 9.3 ± 1.1 | 0.032 | 1053.2 ± 0.6 | 9.3 ± 1.3 |
| 4000 K | 5 | 294.4 ± 1.8 | 7.9 ± 1.3 | 0.027 | 1123 ± 10 | 12.7 ± 3.0 |

- **Loss:** the shear loss in the crystal is about 5–15 % of the liquid value at the same frequency, with tan δ ≈ 0.02–0.05 (Q ≈ 20–60). It is higher at 5000 K than at 4000 K, so it depends on temperature as you would expect for thermally activated relaxation.
- **Storage modulus:** G′ rises with frequency, by about 30 GPa at 5000 K and 17 GPa at 4000 K between 0.2 and 5 THz. Some relaxation is going on in the "perfect-ish" crystal. The 5000 K crystal is a single grain rotated relative to the box, so its moduli are not along the cube axes.
- **At 5 THz:** the second harmonic of the stress is large (|σ₂/σ₁| ≈ 0.27–0.36 in shear). The 5 THz solid points are therefore nonlinear or close to phonon resonance and should not be read as linear-response values. I'll mark them as such in the table.

Still running:
- **Both GK and sweeps:** perfect B1, Schottky and bicrystal.
- **GK only:** all the liquids, 5000 K and 4000 K.
- **One remaining sweep task each:** the liquid uniaxial sweeps at 8000, 7000 and 6000 K.

The final collection of tables and plots runs by itself when the last of these jobs finishes, about 21–23 h from now. The defect runs (Schottky vs. bicrystal vs. perfect B1) will show whether the defects add loss at low frequency.

<details><summary>result of Bash</summary>

```
# Oscillatory deformation NEMD for the complex modulus of MgO, restartable in whole-period chunks.
#   MODE shear    (default): fix deform xz wiggle -> G*(w)
#   MODE uniaxial          : fix deform x  wiggle -> M*(w) = K* + 4/3 G*
# fix nvt/sllod, DeePMD potential.
#
# Required -var:  TEMP (K)  PERIOD (ps, PERIOD*1000 integer)  AMP (xz tilt or lx amplitude, Å)
#                 NTOT (production steps)  NCHUNK (periods per chunk)  NAV (steps per output bin)
#                 SEED  MODEL  DATA  NEQ (equilibration steps)  MAXCHUNKS (chunks this job)
# Optional -var:  TDAMP (production nvt/sllod damping, ps; default 0.1)
# Outputs: stress.dat  (bin averages of the pressure tensor, Nevery=1, Nrepeat=NAV)
#          strain.dat  (instantaneous xz, lx, lz at the same steps; xz may flip by lx)
#          restart.mgo (written after every chunk; the wiggle phase is continuous because
#                       chunks are whole periods and fix deform restarts each run from tilt_start)

units           metal
boundary        p p p
atom_style      atomic
variable        MODE index shear
variable        TDAMP index 0.1   # production thermostat damping (ps)
timestep        0.001

variable        PSTEPS equal round(${PERIOD}*1000)
variable        CHUNK  equal ${NCHUNK}*${PSTEPS}

# restart.mgo: continue production; eq.data: equilibrated, production not started; else start
variable        HAVE_RS equal is_file(restart.mgo)
variable        HAVE_EQ equal is_file(eq.data)
if "${HAVE_RS}" then "read_restart restart.mgo" elif "${HAVE_EQ}" "read_data eq.data" else "read_data ${DATA}"
change_box      all triclinic
mass            1 24.305
mass            2 16.000
neighbor        1.0 bin
neigh_modify    every 1 check yes
pair_style      deepmd ${MODEL}
pair_coeff      * *
timer           timeout ${TIMEOUT} every 1000

thermo          1000
thermo_style    custom step temp press pe pxz xz cpuremain   # pe needed by v_pe

# ---- equilibration (first job only): NVT at TEMP, then reset step so production starts at 0
if "${HAVE_RS} == 0 && ${HAVE_EQ} == 0" then &
   "velocity all create ${TEMP} ${SEED} mom yes rot yes dist gaussian" &
   "fix EQ all nvt temp ${TEMP} ${TEMP} 0.1" &
   "run ${NEQ}" &
   "unfix EQ" &
   "reset_timestep 0" &
   "write_data eq.data"

# ---- production
variable        pxx equal pxx
variable        pyy equal pyy
variable        pzz equal pzz
variable        pxy equal pxy
variable        pxz equal pxz
variable        pyz equal pyz
variable        xz  equal xz
varia
… [2372 more characters]
```

```
# Required -var:  TEMP (K)  PERIOD (ps, PERIOD*1000 integer)  AMP (xz tilt or lx amplitude, Å)
#                 NTOT (production steps)  NCHUNK (periods per chunk)  NAV (steps per output bin)
#                 SEED  MODEL  DATA  NEQ (equilibration steps)  MAXCHUNKS (chunks this job)
# Optional -var:  TDAMP (production nvt/sllod damping, ps; default 0.1)
#                 DT (timestep, ps; default 0.001; PERIOD/DT must be an integer)
# Outputs: stress.dat  (bin averages of the pressure tensor, Nevery=1, Nrepeat=NAV)
#          strain.dat  (instantaneous xz, lx, lz at the same steps; xz may flip by lx)
#          restart.mgo (written after every chunk; the wiggle phase is continuous because
#                       chunks are whole periods and fix deform restarts each run from tilt_start)

units           metal
boundary        p p p
atom_style      atomic
variable        MODE index shear
variable        TDAMP index 0.1   # production thermostat damping (ps)
variable        DT index 0.001      # MD timestep (ps)
timestep        ${DT}

variable        PSTEPS equal round(${PERIOD}/${DT})
variable        CHUNK  equal ${NCHUNK}*${PSTEPS}
6:# Required -var:  TEMP (K)  PERIOD (ps, PERIOD*1000 integer)  AMP (xz tilt or lx amplitude, Å)
```

```
## 7c. Lock-in phase lag: test with half the timestep (2026-10-07)
The perfect B1 crystal (aligned with the box, $G' = C_{44} \approx 171$ GPa) gives these shear phase angles:
- 0.2 THz: $\delta = -0.02°$
- 1 THz: $\delta = -0.20 \pm 0.16°$
- 5 THz: $\delta = -1.47 \pm 0.08°$

The harmonics are small ($|\sigma_2/\sigma_1| < 0.004$), so this is not a nonlinear effect. A negative loss is unphysical.

The phase scales with $\omega$, which points to a constant lag between the stress and the box strain:

$$\delta_{\rm meas} = \delta_{\rm true} - \omega \tau_{\rm lag}, \qquad \tau_{\rm lag} \approx 0.8\ {\rm fs} \approx \Delta t \tag{9}$$

The likely source is the update order. `fix deform` sets the box at `end_of_step`, so the virial at step $n$ is computed in the box of step $n-1$. Meanwhile strain.dat records the box of step $n$.

Size of the correction, $\Delta G'' \approx G' \omega \tau_{\rm lag}$:
- Liquids: under 1 % at $f \le 1$ THz and about 5 % at 5 THz.
- Solids at 5 THz: $\Delta G'' \approx 4$–$7$ GPa, which is the same order as the loss itself.

Test:
- Directory: 5000K/FreqSweep_B1p
```

### Claude (10-07 13:15)

The perfect-B1 sweep at 5000 K is finished, and it turned up a timing error in the measurement of the loss. I've submitted a short test to confirm it (job 4188049).

**Perfect crystal, aligned with the box:**
- **Shear:** G′ = C₄₄ ≈ 171 GPa at all three frequencies, with essentially zero loss at 0.2 and 1 THz. A defect-free lattice has no measurable anelasticity there, as expected.
- **Problem:** at 5 THz the shear loss comes out negative, G″ = −4.4 ± 0.3 GPa (δ = −1.47°). A negative loss is impossible, and the run is linear (|σ₂/σ₁| = 0.002), so nonlinearity doesn't explain it.
- **Likely cause:** the phase error grows in proportion to frequency, which fits a fixed lag of about 0.8 fs, roughly one timestep, between the recorded stress and the recorded box strain. LAMMPS updates the box at the end of each step, so the stress at step n is computed in the previous step's box.
- **Where it matters:** the liquids are hardly affected (under 1 % at ≤ 1 THz, about 5 % at 5 THz). In the solids at 5 THz it shifts G″ by about 4–7 GPa, which is as large as the loss being measured.
- **Uniaxial:** M′ = C₁₁ ≈ 1191–1315 GPa. The 5 THz uniaxial point is strongly nonlinear (|σ₂/σ₁| = 0.42), so I'm leaving it out.

**Crystal grown from the melt (5000 and 4000 K):** this crystal does show loss (tan δ ≈ 0.03–0.05 at 0.2–1 THz) while the perfect crystal shows none. So that loss comes from defects left from solidification, not from the lattice.

**Test:** the perfect crystal at 5 THz again, with the timestep halved to 0.5 fs, about 2.5 GPU-hours. If the lag is real, the negative phase should halve to about −0.7°. I would then correct all phases by ωτ_lag, which matters only for the solid points at 5 THz.

**Changes for the test:**
- I added an optional timestep variable to `anelastic_sweep.lammps`, with the old version backed up. Jobs already running use their own copies, so they are not affected.
- The automatic lock-in on this job will assume 1 fs, so I'll rerun it by hand at 0.5 fs when the job finishes.
- The test is written up in §7c of the restart doc.

All other GK and sweep jobs are still running; the final tables and plots are generated automatically when the last one ends.

### Claude (10-07 13:59)

The Schottky and bicrystal sweeps at 5000 K are finished. The Schottky cell shows the anelastic loss we were looking for; the bicrystal gives a usable result only at 0.2 THz.

Shear results (phases not yet corrected for the timing lag, which adds about +0.06°, +0.3° and +1.5° at 0.2, 1 and 5 THz):

| system | f (THz) | G′ (GPa) | G″ (GPa) | δ |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| perfect B1 | 0.2 / 1 / 5 | 170.5 / 171.3 / 171.8 | ≈ 0 / ≈ 0 / −4.4 (lag) | ≈ 0 |
| Schottky | 0.2 | 148.2 ± 1.6 | 14.1 ± 3.1 | 5.5° |
| Schottky | 1 | 150.0 ± 0.8 | 11.4 ± 2.6 | 4.3° |
| Schottky | 5 | 163 ± 4 | 6.5 ± 6.7 | 2.3° |
| bicrystal | 0.2 | 137.6 ± 1.6 | 6.5 ± 1.1 | 2.7° |
| bicrystal | 1 | 216 ± 27 | 99 ± 58 | 25° |
| bicrystal | 5 | 28 | 33 | 50° |

- **Schottky pairs (about 1 % vacancies):** the loss grows as frequency drops, and G′ softens from 163 to 148 GPa, against 171 GPa (C₄₄) for the perfect crystal. This is the Zener-type relaxation we were looking for. Its loss peak is at or below 0.2 THz, so the pairs take about a picosecond or longer to reorient.
- **Bicrystal:** only the 0.2 THz point is usable (tan δ ≈ 0.05). At 1 and 5 THz the driving frequency is at or above the lowest standing shear wave across the 60 Å slab, about 1 THz. Sliding at the boundaries makes the motion non-uniform, so the response is a size resonance rather than anelasticity. The 1 THz point is noisy and nonlinear, and the 5 THz G′ is mostly inertia. The uniaxial bicrystal values are distorted for the same reason.

**Proposed next runs (not submitted):** shear at periods of 20 ps and 50 ps (0.05 and 0.02 THz) for both the Schottky and bicrystal cells. That would locate the Schottky loss peak and show grain-boundary sliding in the low-frequency range where it is quasi-static. The cost is about 23 P100 GPU-hours, roughly 1 P100-day for the four runs. Do you want me to submit them?

These results are written up in §7d of the restart doc. The other jobs are still running: GK for all the systems, the remaining uniaxial liquid tasks, and the half-timestep test. The final tables and plots are generated automatically when they finish.

```
#!/usr/bin/env python
"""Collect all anelasticity results (oscillatory sweeps + Green-Kubo), tabulate, and plot.

Sweeps: lock-in lines in ROOT/lockin_results.txt (written by sweep_watch.sh); mean T from stress.dat.
GK:     ROOT/pres.dat analysed with gk_viscosity.analyse (block errors).

Viscosity from the slope of the loss modulus (low-frequency limit of a viscoelastic liquid):
    G''(w) = eta_0 w + O(w^3)  ->  eta_0 = lim_{w->0} dG''/dw                       (Eq. 1)
    M''(w) = (zeta_0 + 4/3 eta_0) w + O(w^3)                                         (Eq. 2)
Estimates of dG''/dw:
    secant between neighbouring sweep frequencies  (G''_{i+1} - G''_i)/(w_{i+1} - w_i)  (Eq. 3)
    weighted fit G'' = eta_0 w through the origin over f <= FMAX_FIT                 (Eq. 4)
    GK: d/dw of G''(w) = w int G(t) cos(wt) dt on a fine grid                        (Eq. 5)
Also eta'(w) = G''/w (Eq. 6) and the terminal time tau_0 = lim G'/(w G'') (Eq. 7).

Outputs in OUT: results_all.csv, results_all.md, fig_*.pdf/.png
usage: anelastic_collect.py [--out DIR] [--gk-tc 3] [--fmax-fit 0.2]
```

```
return sec, fit

def gk_analyse(root, tc, tmax=5.0, nblock=8, vbox=4096 * 6.6868):
    fn = f'{root}/pres.dat'
    if not os.path.exists(fn):
        return None
    d = np.loadtxt(fn, comments='#')
    d = d[np.r_[True, np.diff(d[:, 0]) > 0]]
    if len(d) < 50000:
        return None
    dt = np.median(np.diff(d[:, 0])) * 1e-15
    P = d[:, 1:7] * 1e5; T = d[:, 7].mean(); V = vbox * 1e-30
    nlag = int(round(tmax * 1e-12 / dt)) + 1
    res = [gk.analyse(P, T, V, dt, nlag)]
    nb = len(P) // nblock
    res += [gk.analyse(P[i * nb:(i + 1) * nb], d[i * nb:(i + 1) * nb, 7].mean(), V, dt, nlag) for i in range(nblock)]
    fg = np.logspace(-2.5, 1.3, 120); wg = 2 * np.pi * fg * 1e12
    out = dict(T=T, run_ps=len(P) * dt * 1e12, dt=dt, t=np.arange(nlag) * dt * 1e12, f=fg, tc=tc, nblock=nblock,
               p=P[:, :3].mean() / 1e9)
    cum = lambda Rr: np.concatenate([[0], np.cumsum(0.5 * (Rr[1:] + Rr[:-1]))]) * dt
    for k, name in enumerate(('G', 'K', 'M')):
        Rall = [r[k] for r in res]
        out[name] = Rall[0]; out[name + '_int'] = cum(Rall[0])
        ic = int(round(tc * 1e-12 / dt))
        vals = [cum(Rr)[ic] for Rr in Rall[1:]]
        out[name + '0'] = (cum(Rall[0])[ic], np.std(vals, ddof=1) / np.sqrt(nblock))
        sp = [gk.spectrum(Rr, dt, wg, tc * 1e-12, True) for Rr in Rall]
        S = np.array([s[0] for s in sp]); L = np.array([s[1] for s in sp])
        D = np.array([dloss_dw(Rr, dt, wg, tc * 1e-12) for Rr in Rall])       # Eq. 5
        sd = lambda A: np.std(A[1:], axis=0, ddof=1) / np.sqrt(nblock)
        out[name + 's'], out[name + 's_e'] = S[0], sd(S)
        out[name + 'l'], out[name + 'l_e'] = L[0], sd(L)
        out[name + 'd'], out[name + 'd_e'] = D[0], sd(D)
    out['Ginf'] = out['G'][0] / 1e9
    return out

def dloss_dw(R, dt, w, tc):
    """d/dw [w int_0^tc R cos(wt) dt] = int_0^tc R(t) [cos(wt) - wt sin(wt)] dt, same taper as gk.spectrum."""
    t = np.arange(len(R)) * dt
    m = t <= tc
    t, R = t[m], R[m].copy()
    x = np.clip((t - 0.8 * tc) / (0.2 * tc), 0, 1)
    R *= 0.5 * (1 + np.cos(np.pi * x))
    return np.array([np.trapezoid(R * (np.cos(wi * t) - wi * t * np.sin(wi * t)), t) for wi in w])
```

### Claude (10-07 14:43)

Q is now in the results file, the tables and the plots. I regenerated everything in `Anelasticity/results/` with the data so far. The four low-frequency defect runs you approved are running.

**Q in the output:**
- **Formula:** Q⁻¹ = tan δ = G″/G′ (M″/M′ for the uniaxial runs) and Q = 1/tan δ, with propagated errors. The equations are numbered 8–9 in `results_all.md`.
- **Liquids:** at low frequency G′ → 0, so G″/G′ is undefined. I compute Q = G′/G″ directly there, and give an upper bound where G′ isn't resolved.
- **Unresolved loss:** where the loss is below the noise (the perfect crystal in shear), I give a 2σ lower bound, e.g. Q > 180.
- **Tables:**
  - `results_all.md` has a Q column, and a new §1a lists Q at every frequency for each run. Points that are nonlinear or near a resonance (|σ₂/σ₁| > 0.1) are marked †.
  - The Green–Kubo table gives Q = G′/G″ for the liquids only. For the solids and the uniaxial channel the Green–Kubo G′ or M′ is missing its static part, so Q from it would be wrong.
  - `results_all.csv` has Q⁻¹, Q and their errors.
- **Plots:**
  - New `fig_Q`: shear and uniaxial panels, Q⁻¹ on the left axis and Q on the right. Limits are drawn as arrows and nonlinear points get a black ×.
  - The Q panel in `fig_solid` is updated the same way.

**Current Q values (shear):**
- **Liquid:** Q < 1 below about 3 THz.
- **Solid:**

| system | Q at 0.2 THz | Q at 1 THz |
|---|---|---|
| crystal grown from the melt, 5000 K | 32 ± 7 | 21 ± 1 |
| crystal grown from the melt, 4000 K | 64 ± 14 | 31 ± 4 |
| Schottky | 10.5 ± 2.3 | 13 ± 3 |
| bicrystal | 21 ± 4 | resonance |
| perfect B1 | > 180 | > 180 |

The 5 THz solid values are left out because they carry the timing-lag error; the half-timestep test for it is still running.

**New runs:**

| | jobs | period | expected time |
|---|---|---|---|
| Schottky shear | 4195534 | 20 ps and 50 ps | about 5–6.5 h each |
| bicrystal shear | 4195535 | 20 ps and 50 ps | about 5–6.5 h each |

They are watched, and their lock-in results go into the existing sweep files. The final collection waits for these jobs too and adds them to the tables and `fig_Q` automatically.

Notes are in §7d–7e of the restart file.

```
| $\delta$ | −0.48 ± 0.07° |
| $\lvert\sigma_2/\sigma_1\rvert$ | 0.002 |

This value comes from `anelastic_lockin.py --dt 0.0005`. The line that sweep_watch wrote to lockin_results.txt assumed 1 fs and is wrong; ignore it.

Correct each phase by one step, $\omega\Delta t$ = 1.80° (1 fs) and 0.90° (0.5 fs):

$$\delta_{\rm true} = \delta_{\rm meas} + \omega\,\Delta t \tag{11}$$

This gives $+0.33 \pm 0.08°$ and $+0.42 \pm 0.07°$, which agree. The lag is therefore one MD step, and the perfect crystal has a small real phonon loss at 5 THz: $Q \approx 170 \pm 40$.

anelastic_collect.py applies Eq. (10) of its docstring to every sweep point:

$$M^*_{\rm corr} = M^*_{\rm lock\text{-}in}\, e^{i\omega\Delta t} \tag{10}$$

The `--no-lag` option turns the correction off. The lockin_results.txt files themselves remain uncorrected.
```

### Claude (10-07 16:04)

The timing lag is confirmed: the stress lags the strain by exactly one MD timestep. The collection script now corrects every sweep point for it, and the tables and plots in `results/` are regenerated.

**The test:** the perfect B1 crystal in shear at 5 THz, run again with Δt = 0.5 fs.

| Δt | measured δ | after a one-step correction (ωΔt) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 fs | −1.47 ± 0.08° | +0.33 ± 0.08° |
| 0.5 fs | −0.48 ± 0.07° | +0.42 ± 0.07° |

The two corrected values agree. The negative loss came from LAMMPS computing the stress at each step in the previous step's box.

**The correction:** each sweep point is rotated by e^{iωΔt}, with Δt = 1 fs (Eq. 10). The phase shift is 0.07° at 0.2 THz, 0.36° at 1 THz and 1.8° at 5 THz. The raw `lockin_results.txt` files are left uncorrected, and a `--no-lag` switch reproduces the old numbers.

**Effect on the results:**
- **Perfect crystal:** at 5 THz it now has a small positive loss, Q = 173 ± 44, probably phonon–phonon damping. At 0.2 and 1 THz the loss is still not resolved (Q > 120–155).
- **Solids at 5 THz:** the loss went up.

  | crystal | Q before | Q after |
  |---|---|---|
  | grown from the melt, 5000 K | 21 | 12.6 ± 1.1 |
  | grown from the melt, 4000 K | 37 | 17 ± 1.4 |

  Both 5 THz points are still marked nonlinear (|σ₂/σ₁| ≈ 0.3).
- **0.2 and 1 THz:** small changes. Schottky Q is 10.3 ± 2.2 and 12.2 ± 2.5; the crystal grown at 5000 K is 31 ± 7 and 18.7 ± 0.5.
- **Liquids:** G″ rises by about 1–2 GPa at 5 THz, which is negligible.

The test is written up in §7f of the restart file. The low-frequency Schottky and bicrystal runs and the remaining Green–Kubo and liquid jobs are still running, and the final collection runs automatically when they finish.

### Claude (10-07 20:01)

The low-frequency defect runs are finished, but they need care. The Schottky cell turns out not to be stable, and the bicrystal points are too noisy to resolve the loss.

Shear results (the timing-lag correction is ≤ 0.02° at these frequencies):

| cell | f | G′ (GPa) | G″ (GPa) | δ |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Schottky | 50 GHz | 129 ± 5 | 4.8 ± 4.5 | 2.1° |
| Schottky | 20 GHz | 140 ± 9 | 37 ± 19 | 14.8° |
| bicrystal | 50 GHz | 129 ± 8 | 5.8 ± 7.7 | 2.6° |
| bicrystal | 20 GHz | 108 ± 15 | 15 ± 12 | 8.0° |

- **Schottky cell is ageing:**
  - Its energy drops steadily during every run, by about 60 eV, and the pressure falls from 148.5 to 147.2 GPa. In the 50 ps run the mean shear stress jumps by about 1 GPa for part of the run.
  - So the vacancies are annealing or clustering on the time scale of the runs, and the low-frequency Schottky loss mixes reversible relaxation with irreversible change. The 20 GHz point is not a linear modulus.
  - The 0.2–1 THz Schottky values (Q ≈ 10–12) come from the same drifting cell, so treat them with some caution too.
- **Bicrystal is stable:**
  - Energy and pressure are constant.
  - G′ softens steadily as frequency drops: 138 GPa at 0.2 THz, 129 GPa at 50 GHz, 108 GPa at 20 GHz. That fits grain-boundary sliding starting to switch on.
  - G″ is not resolved below 0.2 THz; the errors are 8–15 GPa.
- **Why the errors are large:** stress noise in the solid grows at low frequency, and these runs have only 10–20 cycles.

**Possible follow-ups (not submitted):**
1. **Bicrystal at 20 and 50 ps periods**, with 3× the strain amplitude (γ₀ = 0.03) and 2–3× longer runs. That should cut the errors by about 5×; I would check linearity from the harmonics. About 29 P100-hours.
2. **Schottky cell:** first anneal it at 5000 K until the energy stops falling (about 1 ns), then repeat the 5, 20 and 50 ps periods on the aged cell. About 27 P100-hours.

Together that is about 2.3 P100-days. Do you want either or both?

These results are written up in §7g of the restart doc. Only the Green–Kubo jobs are still running, about 6–14 h more. When they finish, the final collection runs automatically, and these new points will be included.

```
open('$R/FreqSweep_Schottky_aged/sweep.sh','w').write(s)
EOF
```

### Claude (10-08 03:41)

Both follow-up runs are queued, and the plot against temperature with pressure on the right axis is done. Making it turned up a problem in the 6000 K Green–Kubo run, which I've corrected.

**Runs submitted:**
1. **Bicrystal at 3× strain amplitude (γ₀ = 0.03)**, periods of 20 ps and 50 ps (1.0 M and 1.25 M steps). Job 4230008, at most about 13 h per task.
2. **Aged Schottky cell.** The Schottky Green–Kubo run had already aged the cell: its energy fell by 28 eV in the first 150 ps and then stayed flat for 1.35 ns. So no separate anneal was needed. Each task starts from that run's final state, re-equilibrates for 100 ps at 5000 K, and then runs shear at γ₀ = 0.02 with periods of 5, 20 and 50 ps. Job 4230009. The total cost is about the 27 h I quoted, but the time saved on the anneal went into longer runs.

Both are watched, and the final collection will include them.

**New figure, `results/fig_vs_T.pdf/.png`:** four panels against T from 4000 to 8000 K. Each has the mean pressure on the right axis, the melt-grown crystal range shaded, and the 5000 K defect cells as open symbols.
- **(a) Viscosities:** η₀ and ζ₀ from Green–Kubo and from the sweep slopes. η₀ is 8.2, 11.2 and 18.3 mPa·s at 8000, 7000 and 6000 K.
- **(b) Moduli:** G∞ is about 285 GPa at every liquid temperature. The low-frequency M′ jumps from about 650 GPa in the liquid to about 1030 GPa in the crystal.
- **(c) Shear Q:** Q < 1 in the liquid, about 20–60 in the crystal grown from the melt, and > 100 in the perfect crystal.
- **(d) Uniaxial Q:** about 8–40 in the liquid and about 50–130 in the crystal.
- **Pressure:** 192.8, 183.4 and 174.9 GPa for the liquid at 8000, 7000 and 6000 K, then 151.8 and 144.9 GPa for the crystal at 5000 and 4000 K. Freezing drops the pressure by about 23 GPa at fixed volume.

**Corrections:**
- **6000 K Green–Kubo run crystallised** at about 1.05 ns. The energy dropped by 1330 eV and, with no thermostat, the temperature jumped to 8490 K. I now use only the first 1040 ps, which gives η₀ = 18.3 ± 0.5 mPa·s. The 6000 K sweeps stayed liquid, so they are unaffected.
- **Schottky Green–Kubo result is not usable**, even after dropping the ageing period. Vacancy jumps in this small cell cause slow, large steps in the stress, which give an apparent G(0) ≈ 376 GPa. The sweeps are the reliable measurement for this cell.

All tables, the CSV and the figures in `results/` are regenerated, and the details are in §7h of the restart doc. Only the perfect-B1 Green–Kubo run and the two new jobs are still running.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
- **Simulation setup:** LAMMPS + DeePMD (deepmd_py311, graph_c.pb), about 22–27 steps/s on a P100. Oscillatory NEMD uses fix deform wiggle with nvt/sllod, analysed by lock-in. GK uses NVE stress ACFs.
- **Lag correction:** the stress at step n is computed in the box of step n−1, because fix deform updates the box at end_of_step. The fix is M*_corr = M*_lockin · exp(iωΔt) with Δt = 1 fs (Eq. 10 in the collect docstring).
  - Perfect B1 at 5 THz: δ = −1.47° at 1 fs and −0.48° at 0.5 fs, giving +0.33° and +0.42° after correction.
  - The true Q of the perfect crystal at 5 THz is about 173 ± 44.
- **Q definition (Eqs. 8–9):**
  - Q⁻¹ = tan δ = G″/G′ (or M″/M′).
  - For |G″| > |G′| (liquid), Q = G′/G″ is computed directly.
  - Unresolved points get 2σ bounds: "Qmin" means Q > ..., "Qmax" means Q < ....
  - GK Q is given only for liquid shear: the GK M′ lacks K₀ and the solid GK G′ lacks the Born term.
- **Crystallization:** the 6000 K NVE GK run crystallized at about 1050 ps (PE −1330 eV, T → 8490 K). Its analysis is cut at 1040 ps. The 6000 K sweeps stayed liquid.
- **Schottky ageing:** the cell ages over about 150 ps (PE −28 eV in NVE). The sweeps showed a PE drift of about 60–80 eV. Even with 300 ps skipped, the Schottky GK gives G(0) = 376 GPa and ∫G = 289 mPa·s. These come from rare-event stress steps and are unreliable.
- **Bicrystal resonance:** at ≥ 1 THz the bicrystal is near or above the slab transverse resonance, f₁ ≈ v_s/L_z ≈ 1 THz (Eq. 10 in the restart doc §7d), so only the 0.2 THz point and lower are quasi-static.
- **Pressure vs T** (GK ⟨p⟩, fixed V):

| T (K) | 8000 | 7000 | 6000 | 5000 (B1) | 4000 (B1) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ⟨p⟩ (GPa) | 192.8 | 183.4 | 174.9 | 151.8 | 144.9 |

  Freezing at constant V lowers p by about 23 GPa.

```
Read with `REQUIREMENTS.md`, `ANELASTICITY_TODO.md`, `ANELASTICITY_DONE.md`, `ANELASTICITY_PLAN.md`. `ANELASTICITY_RESTART_2026-10-06.md` still holds the GPU fix (§1), sweep design (§2), the full result tables (§3), and the tools (§5); this file adds what changed on 10-07. All LAMMPS paths below are under `/groups/carnegie_poc/rcohen/LAMMPS/MgO/4096atoms/V6.6868/4096atom` (`R`).

## 0. What to check FIRST

1. `squeue -u recohen`:
   - **4115169_5**: uniaxial P = 100 ps, `R/8000K/FreqSweep_x/P100ps_r0`; watcher `tools/sweep_watch.sh R/8000K/FreqSweep_x:4115169:uniaxial` appends to `lockin_results.txt`.
   - **4146463**: GK 8000 K, `R/8000K/GK`.
   - **4146464**: GK 7000 K, `R/7000K/GK`.
   - **4147084** shear sweep 7000 K, `R/7000K/FreqSweep`; **4147085** uniaxial sweep 7000 K, `R/7000K/FreqSweep_x` (§5). Watcher: `tools/sweep_watch.sh R/7000K/FreqSweep:4147084:shear R/7000K/FreqSweep_x:4147085:uniaxial`.
2. GK jobs stopped by the 24 h limit: `sbatch gk.sh` in the same dir (picks up `restart.gk`). Done when `slurm-*.out` shows `PRODUCTION_STEP 1500000 of 1500000`.
3. Analyse: `python tools/gk_viscosity.py <dir>/pres.dat --tmax 5 --nblock 8` (cohen_env). Writes `gk_G.dat`, `gk_spectrum.dat` next to `pres.dat`. Check the NVE drift line and that `msd.dat` grows linearly at 7000 K (liquid). The MSD reference resets at each restart.

## 1. Green–Kubo runs

`tools/gk_stress.lammps`: reads `DATA`, rescales velocities to TEMP, NVT (Nosé–Hoover, 0.1 ps) for NEQ steps, writes `eq.data`, then **NVE** production with the pressure tensor every 2 fs (`pres.dat`: step pxx pyy pzz pxy pxz pyz temp pe etotal, bar). NVE keeps the thermostat out of the stress correlations, which matters for the bulk channel (§3).

| T (K) | start | NEQ | production | job |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8000 | `8000K/FreqSweep/P1ps_r0/eq.data` | 5 ps | 1.5 ns NVE | 4146463 |
| 7000 | same 8000 K `eq.data` | 20 ps NVT at 7000 K | 1.5 ns NVE | 4146464 |

Speed 24.4 steps/s (P100, `graph_c.pb`): ≈ 17 h each, ≈ 1.4 P100-days total.

`tools/gk_viscosity.py` (equations in its docstring):
$$G(t)=\frac{V}{10k_BT}\sum_{\alpha\beta}\langle P^{os}_{\alpha\beta}(0)P^{os}_{\alpha\beta}(t)\rangle \tag{1}$$
$$\eta_0=\int_0^{t_c}G(t)\,dt \tag{2}$$
$$
```

<details><summary>result of Bash</summary>

```
| Schottky | 1 | 150.0 ± 0.8 | 11.4 ± 2.6 | 4.33° | 0.001 |
| Schottky | 5 | 163.0 ± 4.0 | 6.5 ± 6.7 | 2.29° | 0.004 |
| bicrystal Σ5 | 0.2 | 137.6 ± 1.6 | 6.5 ± 1.1 | 2.71° | 0.008 |
| bicrystal | 1 | 216 ± 27 | 99 ± 58 | 24.5° | 0.125 |
| bicrystal | 5 | 28.2 ± 0.4 | 33.2 ± 2.3 | 49.7° | 0.023 |

**Schottky pairs.** The loss is clear and grows toward low frequency. $G'$ falls from 163 to 148 GPa between 5 and 0.2 THz, against $C_{44} = 171$ GPa for the perfect crystal. This is a Zener-type relaxation whose loss peak lies at or below 0.2 THz, so the relaxation time is of order 1 ps or longer.

**Bicrystal.** Only the 0.2 THz point is quasi-static. At 1 and 5 THz the driving is close to, or above, the lowest transverse standing wave across the slab:

$$f_1 \approx \frac{v_s}{L_z} \approx \frac{5.9\ {\rm km/s}}{59.6\ {\rm \AA}} \approx 1\ {\rm THz} \tag{10}$$

The compliant boundaries lower this frequency further. Sliding at the boundaries makes the motion non-affine, so inertia enters the response. The results are a finite-size resonance (large errors and harmonics at 1 THz, inertia-dominated $G'$ at 5 THz), not anelasticity.

The uniaxial bicrystal values are non-monotonic: $M' = 977$, 889 and 1174 GPa. The cause is the same.

**Next step (proposed, not submitted):** shear at $P = 20$ and 50 ps for the Schottky and bicrystal cells, to find the loss peak and grain-boundary sliding.

**Submitted (RC approved, 2026-10-07):** tasks 7–8 (P20ps_xz, P50ps_xz) were appended to tasks.txt in FreqSweep_Schottky (job 4195534) and FreqSweep_bicrystal (job 4195535).

| period | steps | cycles | NAV | NCHUNK |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 20 ps | 400k | 20 | 100 | 5 |
| 50 ps | 500k | 10 | 250 | 2 |

- Shear strain amplitude: $\gamma_0 = 0.01$.
- Watched by sweep_watch.sh.
- Expected run time: about 5–6.5 h each.

## 7e. Quality factor Q in the collection (2026-10-07)
anelastic_collect.py now reports $Q$ (Eqs. 8–9 in its docstring and in results_all.md):
- **Formula:** $Q^{-1} = \tan\delta = G''/G'$ (or $M''/M'$), and $Q = 1/\tan\delta$.
- **Liquid-like points:** where $|G''| > |G'|$, $Q = G'/G''$ is computed directly, because $G' \to 0$ in a liquid.
- **Unresolved points:** reported as 2σ bounds.
- **CSV:** new columns Qinv, dQinv, Q, dQ, Q_text.
- **Tables:** the sweep table has a $Q$ column, and the new §1a gives $Q$ at every frequency (marked † when $|\sigma_2/\sigma_1| > 0.1$). The GK table has $Q = G'/G''$ for the liquids only.
- **Figures:**
  - New fig_Q has $Q^{-1}$
```

```
# Oscillatory deformation NEMD for the complex modulus of MgO, restartable in whole-period chunks.
#   MODE shear    (default): fix deform xz wiggle -> G*(w)
#   MODE uniaxial          : fix deform x  wiggle -> M*(w) = K* + 4/3 G*
# fix nvt/sllod, DeePMD potential.
#
# Required -var:  TEMP (K)  PERIOD (ps, PERIOD*1000 integer)  AMP (xz tilt or lx amplitude, Å)
#                 NTOT (production steps)  NCHUNK (periods per chunk)  NAV (steps per output bin)
#                 SEED  MODEL  DATA  NEQ (equilibration steps)  MAXCHUNKS (chunks this job)
# Optional -var:  TDAMP (production nvt/sllod damping, ps; default 0.1)
#                 DT (timestep, ps; default 0.001; PERIOD/DT must be an integer)
# Outputs: stress.dat  (bin averages of the pressure tensor, Nevery=1, Nrepeat=NAV)
#          strain.dat  (instantaneous xz, lx, lz at the same steps; xz may flip by lx)
#          restart.mgo (written after every chunk; the wiggle phase is continuous because
#                       chunks are whole periods and fix deform restarts each run from tilt_start)

units           metal
boundary        p p p
atom_style      atomic
variable        MODE index shear
variable        TDAMP index 0.1   # production thermostat damping (ps)
variable        DT index 0.001      # MD timestep (ps)
timestep        ${DT}

variable        PSTEPS equal round(${PERIOD}/${DT})
variable        CHUNK  equal ${NCHUNK}*${PSTEPS}

# restart.mgo: continue production; eq.data: equilibrated, production not started; else start
variable        HAVE_RS equal is_file(restart.mgo)
variable        HAVE_EQ equal is_file(eq.data)
if "${HAVE_RS}" then "read_restart restart.mgo" elif "${HAVE_EQ}" "read_data eq.data" else "read_data ${DATA}"
change_box      all triclinic
mass            1 24.305
mass            2 16.000
neighbor        1.0 bin
neigh_modify    every 1 check yes
pair_style      deepmd ${MODEL}
pair_coeff      * *
timer           timeout ${TIMEOUT} every 1000

thermo          1000
thermo_style    custom step temp press pe pxz xz cpuremain   # pe needed by v_pe

# ---- equilibration (first job only): NVT at TEMP, then reset step
```

```
# Equilibrium MD for Green-Kubo viscosities of MgO (DeePMD), restartable in chunks.
# Start: read DATA (with velocities), NVT at TEMP for NEQ steps (Nose-Hoover, 0.1 ps), then NVE production.
# Production is NVE so that no thermostat enters the stress correlations (shear or bulk).
#
# Required -var: TEMP (K)  DATA  MODEL  NEQ  NTOT (production steps)  NCHUNK (steps per chunk)
#                NS (sampling interval, steps)  MAXCHUNKS  TIMEOUT (s)
# Outputs: pres.dat    step pxx pyy pzz pxy pxz pyz temp pe etotal  (instantaneous, every NS steps, bar)
#          msd.dat     step msd(Mg) msd(O)  (every 1000 steps; checks that the liquid stays liquid)
#          eq.data     end of NVT equilibration;  restart.gk  after every chunk

units           metal
boundary        p p p
atom_style      atomic
timestep        0.001

variable        HAVE_RS equal is_file(restart.gk)
variable        HAVE_EQ equal is_file(eq.data)
if "${HAVE_RS}" then "read_restart restart.gk" elif "${HAVE_EQ}" "read_data eq.data" else "read_data ${DATA}"
mass            1 24.305
mass            2 16.000
neighbor        1.0 bin
neigh_modify    every 1 check yes
pair_style      deepmd ${MODEL}
pair_coeff      * *
timer           timeout ${TIMEOUT} every 1000

thermo          1000
thermo_style    custom step temp press pe etotal pxy cpuremain

# ---- equilibration (first job only), from the velocities in DATA
if "${HAVE_RS} == 0 && ${HAVE_EQ} == 0" then &
   "velocity all scale ${TEMP}" &
   "fix EQ all nvt temp ${TEMP} ${TEMP} 0.1" &
   "run ${NEQ}" &
   "unfix EQ" &
   "reset_timestep 0" &
   "write_data eq.data"

# ---- NVE production
fix             NVE all nve
group           Mg type 1
group           O  type 2
compute         msdMg Mg msd com yes
compute         msdO  O  msd com yes
variable        pxx equal pxx
variable        pyy equal pyy
variable        pzz equal pzz
variable        pxy equal pxy
variable        pxz equal pxz
variable        pyz equal pyz
variable        tt  equal temp
variable        pe  equal pe
variable        et  equal etotal
variable        mMg equal c_msdMg[4]
variable        mO  equal c_msdO[4]
fix             PAV all ave/time ${NS} 1 ${NS} v_pxx v_pyy v_pzz v_pxy v_pxz v_pyz v_tt v_pe v_et append pres.dat
fix             MAV all ave/time 1000 1 1000 v_mMg v_mO append msd.dat

variable        NXT equal ternary((floor(step/${NCHUNK})+1)*${NCHUNK}<${NTOT},(floor(step/${NCHUNK})+1)*${NCHUNK},${NTOT})
label           loop
variable        ic loop ${MAXCHUNKS}
  if "$(
```

<details><summary>result of Bash</summary>

```
#!/usr/bin/env python
"""Collect all anelasticity results (oscillatory sweeps + Green-Kubo), tabulate, and plot.

Sweeps: lock-in lines in ROOT/lockin_results.txt (written by sweep_watch.sh); mean T from stress.dat.
GK:     ROOT/pres.dat analysed with gk_viscosity.analyse (block errors).

Viscosity from the slope of the loss modulus (low-frequency limit of a viscoelastic liquid):
    G''(w) = eta_0 w + O(w^3)  ->  eta_0 = lim_{w->0} dG''/dw                       (Eq. 1)
    M''(w) = (zeta_0 + 4/3 eta_0) w + O(w^3)                                         (Eq. 2)
Estimates of dG''/dw:
    secant between neighbouring sweep frequencies  (G''_{i+1} - G''_i)/(w_{i+1} - w_i)  (Eq. 3)
    weighted fit G'' = eta_0 w through the origin over f <= FMAX_FIT                 (Eq. 4)
    GK: d/dw of G''(w) = w int G(t) cos(wt) dt on a fine grid                        (Eq. 5)
Also eta'(w) = G''/w (Eq. 6) and the terminal time tau_0 = lim G'/(w G'') (Eq. 7).
Quality factor and internal friction (both channels; M for uniaxial):
    Q^-1 = tan(delta) = G''/G',  Q = G'/G''                                         (Eq. 8)
    sigma(Q^-1)^2 = (sigma_G''/G')^2 + (G'' sigma_G'/G'^2)^2,  sigma_Q = sigma(Q^-1)/Q^-2   (Eq. 9)
    For |G''| > |G'| (liquid-like, Q < 1) the same is done for Q = G'/G'' directly, sigma_Q^2 = (sigma_G'/G'')^2 + (G' sigma_G''/G''^2)^2.
    Unresolved points get 2-sigma bounds: Q > 1/(max(Q^-1,0) + 2 sigma) (loss unresolved) or Q < max(Q,0) + 2 sigma_Q (storage unresolved).
GK gives Q(w) = G'/G'' only for the liquid shear channel (equilibrium G = 0); the longitudinal GK M' lacks the
relaxed K_0, and the solid GK G' lacks the Born term, so no GK Q is given for those.

Lag correction: the stress recorded at step n is computed in the box of step n-1 (fix deform updates the box at
end_of_step), so the lock-in phase is low by exactly one MD step (perfect B1 at 5 THz: delta = -1.47 deg at
dt = 1 fs and -0.48 deg at 0.5 fs; both give +0.38 deg after the correction). Every sweep point is rotated by
    M*_corr = M*_lockin exp(i w dt_MD),  dt_MD = 1 fs                                 (Eq. 10)
i.e. M'' -> M'' cos(w dt) + M' sin(w dt), M' -> M' cos(w dt) - M'' sin(w dt). --no-lag turns it off.

Outputs in OUT: results_all.csv, results_all.md, fig_*.pdf/.png
usage: anelastic_collect.py [--out DIR] [--gk-tc 3] [--fmax-fit 0.2]
"""
import argparse, os, re, sys
import numpy as np
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
import gk_viscosity as gk
import
… [11261 more characters]
```

```
def fig_vs_T(S, G, out):
    """Fig 8: results versus temperature (melt-grown series 4000-8000 K; defect cells as extra points at 5000 K),
    each panel with the mean pressure <p>(T) of the NVE GK runs (fixed V = 6.6868 Å^3/atom) on the right axis."""
    Tg = sorted((int(k), g) for k, g in G.items() if g is not None and k.isdigit())
    if not Tg:
        return
    TP = np.array([t for t, _ in Tg]); PP = np.array([g['p'] for _, g in Tg])
    by = {s['lab']: s for s in S}

    def at(s, f0):                                  # sweep row at frequency f0 (Hz), or None
        if s is None:
            return None
        r = [r for r in s['rows'] if abs(r['f'] / f0 - 1) < 0.01]
        return r[0] if r else None

    def pressure(ax):
        ax2 = ax.twinx()
        ax2.plot(TP, PP, '-', color='0.35', lw=2.2, marker='D', ms=7, mfc='white', zorder=0, label=r'$\langle p\rangle$ (GK, NVE)')
        ax2.set_ylabel('Pressure (GPa)', color='0.3'); ax2.tick_params(axis='y', colors='0.3')
        ax.set_zorder(ax2.get_zorder() + 1); ax.patch.set_visible(False)
        return ax2

    def melt(ax, ax2):
        ax2.axvspan(3800, 5500, color='0.92', zorder=-5, lw=0, label='B1 crystal (melt-grown); white: liquid')

    DEF = [('5000 B1 ref', 'perfect B1', 'D'), ('5000 Schottky', 'Schottky', 'v'), ('5000 Schottky aged', 'Schottky aged', 'v'),
           ('5000 bicrystal', 'bicrystal', 'P')]
    fig, axs = plt.subplots(2, 2, figsize=(15, 11.5)); fig.subplots_adjust(wspace=0.55, hspace=0.42)
    leg2 = None

    # (a) viscosities
    ax = axs[0, 0]
    liq = [(t, g) for t, g in Tg if str(t) in LIQ]
    sol = [(t, g) for t, g in Tg if str(t) not in LIQ]
    for key, lab, mk, c in (('G0', r'$\eta_0$ GK (Eq. 2)', 'o', '#1f5fa8'), ('K0', r'$\zeta_0$ GK (Eq. 6)', 's', '#c0392b')):
        ax.errorbar([t for t, _ in liq], [g[key][0] * 1e3 for _, g in liq], [g[key][1] * 1e3 for _, g in liq],
                    fmt=mk + '-', color=c, ms=9, capsize=3, label=lab)
        if sol:
            ax.errorbar([t for t, _ in sol], [g[key][0] * 1e3 for _, g in sol], [g[key][1] * 1e3 for _, g in sol],
                        fmt=mk, mfc='white', color=c, ms=9, capsize=3, label=lab.split(' GK')[0] + ' solid: ∫ dt (loss, not viscosity)')
    for kind, lab, mk, c in (('shear', r'$\eta_0$ sweep slope (Eq. 4)', '^', '#2a8a4a'), ('uniax', r'$\zeta_0$ + 4/3 $\eta_0$ sweep slope (Eq. 4)', 'v', '#d4870f')):
```

```
158:def gk_analyse(root, tc, tmax=5.0, nblock=8, vbox=None, cut_ps=None, skip_ps=None):
172:    P = d[:, 1:7] * 1e5; T = d[:, 7].mean(); V = vbox * 1e-30
346:    G = {k: gk_analyse(root, a.gk_tc, vbox=VBOX.get(k, NATOM * VATOM), cut_ps=GKCUT.get(k), skip_ps=GKSKIP.get(k)) for k, root in GKS}
def gk_analyse(root, tc, tmax=5.0, nblock=8, vbox=None, cut_ps=None, skip_ps=None):
    fn = f'{root}/pres.dat'
    if not os.path.exists(fn):
        return None
    d = np.loadtxt(fn, comments='#')
    d = d[np.r_[True, np.diff(d[:, 0]) > 0]]
    t0 = (d[:, 0] - d[0, 0]) * 1e-3
    if cut_ps is not None:
        d, t0 = d[t0 <= cut_ps], t0[t0 <= cut_ps]
    if skip_ps is not None:
        d = d[t0 >= skip_ps]
    if len(d) < 50000:
        return None
    dt = np.median(np.diff(d[:, 0])) * 1e-15
    P = d[:, 1:7] * 1e5; T = d[:, 7].mean(); V = vbox * 1e-30
    nlag = int(round(tmax * 1e-12 / dt)) + 1
    res = [gk.analyse(P, T, V, dt, nlag)]
    nb = len(P) // nblock
    res += [gk.analyse(P[i * nb:(i + 1) * nb], d[i * nb:(i + 1) * nb, 7].mean(), V, dt, nlag) for i in range(nblock)]
    fg = np.logspace(-2.5, 1.3, 120); wg = 2 * np.pi * fg * 1e12
```

```
< Rows 5000, 4000, 5000 B1 ref, 5000 Schottky and 5000 bicrystal are **crystalline** (B1). There, $G(0)=V\langle\delta\sigma^2\rangle/k_BT$ is only the fluctuation part of the modulus (the Born term is missing), and $\int G\,dt$ is the low-frequency loss from phonon damping and defects ($G''\approx\omega\int G\,dt$), not a viscosity. The elastic $G'$ of the solid comes from the sweeps.
```

**Tool `Bash`** — Write a launcher that submits sweeps once GK equilibration is done

```
cat > /resnick/home/recohen/Claude/tools/launch_after_eq.sh <<'EOF'
#!/bin/bash
# Wait until the start file (DATA, tasks.txt column 16) of every task of a sweep root exists, copy it into each task
# dir as eq.data (production starts from it, no re-equilibration), submit the array, then watch it with sweep_watch.sh.
# usage: launch_after_eq.sh ROOT JOBNAME          (run in the background; prints the job id, then watcher lines)
ROOT=$1; NAME=$2
N=$(grep -vc '^#' $ROOT/tasks.txt)
while true; do
  ok=1
  for f in $(grep -v '^#' $ROOT/tasks.txt | awk '{print $16}' | sort -u); do [ -s $f ] || ok=0; done
  [ $ok -eq 1 ] && break
  sleep 120
done
sleep 30                                             # let write_data finish
grep -v '^#' $ROOT/tasks.txt | while read D x x x x x x x x x x x x x x DATA; do
  mkdir -p $ROOT/$D; [ -f $ROOT/$D/eq.data ] || cp $DATA $ROOT/$D/eq.data
done
JOB=$(cd $ROOT && sbatch --parsable -J $NAME --array=1-$N sweep_array.sh)
echo "SUBMITTED $ROOT $NAME job $JOB ($N tasks)"
exec /resnick/home/recohen/Claude/tools/sweep_watch.sh $ROOT:$JOB:shear
EOF
chmod +x /resnick/home/recohen/Claude/tools/launch_after_eq.sh; echo ok
```

```
# MgO anelasticity and viscosity from oscillatory-shear NEMD

Started 2026-10-05. System: MgO, 4096 atoms, $V = 6.6868$ Å$^3$/atom (constant volume, about 190 GPa at 8000 K), DeePMD potential `graph.pb` (Mg, O; $r_c = 7$ Å), LAMMPS `~/bin/lmp` (22Jul2025) with conda env `deepmd_py311`, Tesla P100.

## 1. Quantities

Imposed shear strain and measured stress (sign: $\sigma_{xz} = -P_{xz}$):

$$\gamma(t) = \gamma_0 \sin\omega t, \qquad \gamma = \mathrm{xz}/L_z \tag{1}$$

$$\sigma(t) = \gamma_0\,[\,G'(\omega)\sin\omega t + G''(\omega)\cos\omega t\,] + \text{noise} \tag{2}$$

$$G^*(\omega) = G' + iG'', \qquad \tan\delta = G''/G' = Q^{-1}, \qquad \eta'(\omega) = G''/\omega \tag{3}$$

In Gross's framework the complex modulus is generated by a relaxation spectrum $H(\tau)$:

$$G^*(\omega) = G_e + \int_{-\infty}^{\infty} H(\tau)\,\frac{i\omega\tau}{1+i\omega\tau}\,d\ln\tau \tag{4}$$

with relaxation function $G(t) = G_e + \int H(\tau)e^{-t/\tau}\,d\ln\tau$ and $G^*(\omega) = i\omega\int_0^\infty G(t)e^{-i\omega t}dt$. Green–Kubo gives $G(t) = (V/k_BT)\langle\sigma_{xz}(0)\sigma_{xz}(t)\rangle$ directly, so it is the equilibrium cross-check of Eq. (4).

A uniaxial $x$ wiggle (run R1A1) changes volume and measures the longitudinal modulus:

$$M^* = K^* + \tfrac{4}{3}G^*, \qquad M''/\omega = \zeta + \tfrac{4}{3}\eta \tag{5}$$

## 2. Analysis: lock-in at the known drive frequency

`~/Claude/tools/anelastic_lockin.py` fits stress and strain to $a + bt + \sum_{k=1}^{3}[s_k\sin k\omega t + c_k\cos k\omega t]$ and forms $G^* = \hat\sigma_1/\hat\gamma_1$. Error bars come from whole-cycle blocks. Harmonics $|\hat\sigma_2/\hat\sigma_1|$, $|\hat\sigma_3/\hat\sigma_1|$ flag nonlinearity. Stresses written as $N_{av}$-step trailing averages are corrected exactly by

$$H(\omega) = \frac{\sin(N_{av}\omega\Delta t/2)}{N_{av}\sin(\omega\Delta t/2)}\,e^{-i\omega(N_{av}-1)\Delta t/2} \tag{6}$$

Box flips at $|\mathrm{xz}| > L_x/2$ are unwrapped, so strain amplitudes above 0.5 are allowed.

Reanalysis of the existing 8000 K runs (1 THz):

| run | $\gamma_0$ | modulus | $\delta$ | loss$/\omega$ (Pa s) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 54671682, xz shear | 0.0066 | $G'=15.5\pm3.6$, $G''=42.9\pm4.9$ GPa | 70° | $\eta' = 0.0068\pm0.0008$ |
| R1A1, x uniaxial | 0.033 | $M'=537.5\pm1.6$, $M''=89.1\pm0.9$ GPa | 9.4° | $\zeta+\frac43\eta = 0.0142\pm0.0001$ |

A Maxwell fit to the shear point gives $G_\infty \approx 135$ GPa, $\tau \approx 0.057$ ps, $\eta_0 \approx 7.7$ mPa s.

## 3. Noise and run leng
… [8572 more characters]
```

### Claude (10-08 06:56)

The documentation is written, the V9.6397 runs are underway, and sample trajectories are running at the original volume (V6.6868). First, a correction: the aged-Schottky job I reported as queued last time (4230009) had actually failed within seconds. The file-conversion step crashed because it set no interatomic potential. I fixed that and resubmitted it as 4233462, and all three tasks are now running.

**Documentation**
- `ANELASTICITY_METHODS.md` is a standalone guide for a future session or another material. It covers:
  - the physics, with numbered equations (lock-in, Q, Green–Kubo, the one-step lag correction, the thermostat artefact, heating, slab resonance);
  - the protocol that worked, every tool and how to call it, job and environment rules, measured speeds and costs, and all the pitfalls found;
  - a porting checklist. For example, atomic masses are hard-coded in the LAMMPS inputs, and the defect builder only does rock-salt.
- `ANELASTICITY_RESTART_2026-10-08.md` lists the job IDs and what to check first. TODO, DONE and memory are updated.
- The collection script no longer has V6.6868 hard-coded. Each volume or material gets its own case file (`anelastic_cases_MgO_V6.6868.py` and `..._V9.6397.py`). The V6.6868 output is unchanged.

**Trajectories**
- The sweep input now takes `DUMPN`/`DUMPSTEPS`, which save a trajectory only for the first part of a run.
- Eight sample runs at V6.6868 are going (job 4233485), each 3–5 cycles and about 75 MB:
  - liquid 8000 K: shear at 20 ps and 1 ps periods, and uniaxial at 5 ps;
  - 5000 K crystals at a 20 ps period: perfect B1, aged Schottky and bicrystal;
  - two extra bicrystal runs: one at γ₀ = 0.1 so the boundary sliding is visible by eye, and one at the 1 ps resonance.
- Two have finished and contain 501 frames each.
- In OVITO, the solid strains (0.01–0.03) are too small to see directly. Show them with displacement vectors scaled up, or with atomic strain.

**V9.6397 runs**
Your earlier runs there show liquid down to 4000 K (≈ 38 GPa) and crystallization at 3000 K (≈ 18 GPa). The same DeePMD model was used, so the compressed version is reused. I submitted your choice:
- **Green–Kubo runs** for the 6000 and 4000 K liquids, the 3000 K melt-grown crystal, and the perfect, Schottky and bicrystal cells at 3000 K (jobs 4233549–54).
- **Starting states:** your `Liq8000.in.pos`, and your 4000 K `end.pos` for both the 4000 K run and the 3000 K melt-grown run. The melt-grown run gets 300 ps of NVT to crystallize.
- **Sweeps** submit automatically as each run finishes equilibrating. Strains are the same as at V6.6868, with amplitudes scaled to the larger box. The low-frequency 20 and 50 ps defect runs are included, and the 20 ps shear tasks save trajectories.
- **Waiting on a check:** the 3000 K melt-grown sweep and the 2000 K run, which starts from the 3000 K crystal. I'll confirm the crystal formed (energy drop and plateau, Bragg peaks) before launching them.
- **Cost:** it runs at about 32 steps/s here versus 24 at V6.6868, so the total is about 9 P100-days instead of 12. Each 1.5 ns Green–Kubo run takes about 13 h.

**Bicrystal cell fix:** the bicrystal builder's overlap cutoff didn't scale with the lattice constant, which left atoms 1.35 Å apart at this volume. It now scales, and the rebuilt cell has the same 3920-atom structure as at V6.6868.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
- **Simulation setup:** LAMMPS + DeePMD (deepmd_py311, graph_c.pb; same model md5 81f3fc48 at both volumes).
  - Oscillatory NEMD uses fix deform wiggle with nvt/sllod and lock-in analysis.
  - GK uses NVE stress autocorrelations.
- **Corrections and protocol rules:**
  - Lag correction: exp(iωΔt).
  - Q handling: liquid-like points and 2σ bounds.
  - Uniaxial thermostat rule: large τ_T (adiabatic).
  - Bicrystal slab resonance: f₁ ≈ v_s/L_z.
  - Schottky cells age; check PE stationarity.
  - Crystallization can happen during the NVE GK run (cut the analysis with GKCUT).
- **V9.6397 cell:** L = 34.051872 Å, B1 a = 4.256484 Å.

| 6000 | ≈ 50 | liquid |
| 5000 | ≈ 44 | liquid |
| 4000 | ≈ 38 | liquid, 1 ns, etot −18260 eV |
| 3000 | ≈ 18 | crystallized |

- **Speed at V9.6397:** about 32 steps/s (V6.6868: 24), so 1.5 ns GK ≈ 13 h. Total V9 cost ≈ 25.6 M steps ≈ 220 P100-h ≈ 9 P100-days.

**/resnick/home/recohen/Claude/tools/sweep_array.sh** (new, generic P100 array job)
- Reads the tasks.txt columns `D TEMP PERIOD AMP NTOT NCHUNK NAV SEED NEQ MAXCHUNKS MODE TDAMP [DT DUMPN DUMPSTEPS DATA]`.
- Defaults: DT 0.001, DUMPN 0, DUMPSTEPS 0, DATA $ROOT/start.data. A relative DATA is resolved against ROOT.
- `mkdir -p $ROOT/$D; cd $ROOT/$D`.
- If DATA is a restart (*.gk, *.mgo, *.restart), it writes restart2data.in (read_restart, `pair_style zero 6.0`, `pair_coeff * *`, `write_data ${OUT} nocoeff`) and converts it to start.data.
- Runs lmp with all the variables and TIMEOUT 86100.
- Submit with `sbatch -J NAME --array=1-N sweep_array.sh`.

**/resnick/home/recohen/Claude/tools/launch_after_eq.sh ROOT NAME** (new)
- Waits until every task's DATA (column 16) exists, sleeps 30 s, then copies DATA into each task directory as eq.data.
- Submits `sbatch --parsable -J $NAME --array=1-$N sweep_array.sh`, prints SUBMITTED, then execs `sweep_watch.sh $ROOT:$JOB:shear`.
